**Seattle** ([/siˈætəl/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/6e/GT_Seattle_AE.ogg/GT_Seattle_AE.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/6e/GT_Seattle_AE.ogg/GT_Seattle_AE.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> [*see-AT-əl*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key)) is the [most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Washington) in the U.S. state of [Washington](13015878.md) and the [Pacific Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Northwest) region of [North America](21139.md). It is the [18th-most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) in the United States with a population of 784,777 in 2025,[^cite_note-2025_Quickfacts-5] while the [Seattle metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_metropolitan_area) at over 4.15 million residents is the [15th-most populous metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_statistical_areas) in the nation.[^cite_note-14] The city is the [county seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_seat) of [King County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_County,_Washington), the [most populous county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Washington) in Washington. Seattle's growth rate of 21.1% between 2010 and 2020 made it one of the country's fastest-growing large cities.[^cite_note-15]

Seattle is situated on an [isthmus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isthmus) between [Puget Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puget_Sound), an inlet of the [Pacific Ocean](23070.md), and [Lake Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Washington). It is the northernmost major city in the United States, located about 100 miles (160 km) south of the [Canadian border](630260.md). A gateway for trade with the [Asia-Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific) region, the [Port of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Seattle) is the fourth-largest port in North America in terms of container handling as of 2021.[^cite_note-16]

The Seattle area has been inhabited by [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) (such as the [Duwamish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwamish_people), who had at least 17 villages around [Elliott Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Bay)) for at least 4,000 years before the arrival of the first permanent European settlers.[^cite_note-Discovery_Park-17] [Arthur A. Denny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_A._Denny) and his group of travelers, subsequently known as the [Denny Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denny_Party), arrived from [Illinois](14849.md) via [Portland, Oregon](23503.md), on the [schooner *Exact*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schooner_Exact) at [Alki Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alki_Point,_Seattle) on November 13, 1851.[^cite_note-SonsOfTheProfits-18] The settlement was moved to the eastern shore of Elliott Bay in 1852 and named "Seattle" in honor of [Chief Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Seattle), a prominent 19th-century leader of the local Duwamish and [Suquamish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suquamish_people) tribes. Seattle has relatively high populations of Native Americans as well as Americans with strong Asian, African, and European ancestry, and, as of 2015, has the fifth-highest percentage of residents who identify as [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_culture_in_Seattle) [among major metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_demographics_of_the_United_States#By_metropolitan_area) in the U.S. (4.8 percent).[^cite_note-Fifth2015-19]

[Logging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logging) was Seattle's first major industry, but by the late 19th century the city had become a commercial and shipbuilding center as a gateway to [Alaska](624.md) during the [Klondike Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klondike_Gold_Rush). The city grew after [World War II](32927.md), partly due to the local company [Boeing](18933266.md), which established Seattle as a center for its manufacturing of aircraft. Beginning in the 1980s, the Seattle area developed into a [technology center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_technology_centers); [Microsoft](19001.md) established its headquarters in the region. [Alaska Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Airlines) is based at [Seattle–Tacoma International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle%E2%80%93Tacoma_International_Airport) in nearby [SeaTac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeaTac,_Washington). The rise of new software, [biotechnology](4502.md), and Internet companies led to an economic revival, which increased the city's population by almost 50,000 between 1990 and 2000.

The culture of Seattle is heavily defined by its [significant musical history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Seattle). Between 1918 and 1951, nearly 24 [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) nightclubs existed along Jackson Street, from the current [Chinatown/International District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown/International_District,_Seattle) to the [Central District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_District,_Seattle). The jazz scene nurtured the early careers of [Ernestine Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernestine_Anderson), [Ray Charles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Charles), [Quincy Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Jones), and others. In the late 20th and early 21st centuries, the city was the origin of several [rock](25423.md) artists and bands, including the [Foo Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foo_Fighters), [Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_\(band\)), and [Jimi Hendrix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimi_Hendrix), and the subgenre of [grunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grunge) and its pioneering bands, including [Alice in Chains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_in_Chains), [Nirvana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nirvana_\(band\)), [Pearl Jam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Jam), and [Soundgarden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundgarden).[^cite_note-Seattle_Sound-20]

## History

Main article: [History of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Seattle)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Seattle).

[Archaeological excavations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_excavation) suggest that [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) have inhabited the Seattle area for at least 4,000 years.[^cite_note-Discovery_Park-17] By the time the first European settlers arrived, the [Duwamish people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwamish_people) occupied at least 17 villages in the areas around [Elliott Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Bay).[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] The name for the modern city of Seattle in [Lushootseed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lushootseed), *dᶻidᶻəlal̓ič*, meaning "little crossing-over place", comes from one of these villages, which was located at the present-day [King Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Street_Station).[^cite_note-BatesHess1994-24][^cite_note-Waterman2001-25][^cite_note-HuntKaylor1917-26]

In May 1792, [George Vancouver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Vancouver) was the first European to visit the Seattle area during his [1791–1795 expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver_Expedition) for the [Royal Navy](26061.md), which sought to chart the [Pacific Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Northwest) for the British.[^cite_note-Vancouver1801-27]

### 19th century



[Seattle's first streetcar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Street_Railway) at the corner of Occidental and Yesler in 1884; all buildings depicted were destroyed by the [Great Seattle Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Seattle_Fire) five years later, in 1889.



An 1891 aerial view of Seattle and surrounding [King County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_County,_Washington)

[^cite_note-28]

In 1851, a large party of [American pioneers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_pioneer) led by Luther Collins made a location on land at the mouth of the [Duwamish River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwamish_River); they formally claimed it on September 14, 1851.[^cite_note-29] Thirteen days later, members of the Collins Party on the way to their claim passed three scouts of the Denny Party.[^cite_note-30] Members of the Denny Party claimed land on [Alki Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alki_Point,_Seattle) on September 28, 1851.[^cite_note-founding-31] The rest of the Denny Party set sail on the [schooner *Exact*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schooner_Exact) from [Portland](23503.md), Oregon, stopping in [Astoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astoria,_Oregon), and landed at Alki Point during a rainstorm on November 13, 1851.[^cite_note-founding-31] After a difficult winter, most of the Denny Party relocated across Elliott Bay and claimed land a second time at the site of present-day [Pioneer Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Square,_Seattle),[^cite_note-founding-31] naming this new settlement *Duwamps*.[^cite_note-32]

Charles Terry and John Low remained at the original landing location, reestablished their old land claim and called it "New York", but renamed **"New York Alki"** in April 1853, from a [Chinook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinook_Jargon) word meaning, roughly, "by and by" or "someday".[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-ws-34] For the next few years, New York Alki and Duwamps competed for dominance, but in time Alki was abandoned and its residents moved across the bay to join the rest of the settlers.[^cite_note-35]

[David Swinson "Doc" Maynard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Swinson_Maynard), one of the founders of Duwamps, was the primary advocate to name the settlement after [Chief Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Seattle) ([Lushootseed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lushootseed_language): *siʔaɫ*, anglicized as "Seattle"), chief of the Duwamish and [Suquamish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suquamish_people) tribes.[^cite_note-nameorigin-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] The brief [Puget Sound War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puget_Sound_War) culminated in the [Battle of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Seattle_\(1856\)) on January 26, 1856. The attack was repelled, and the settlement was never attacked again.[^cite_note-39]

The name "Seattle" appears on official [Washington Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Territory) papers dated May 23, 1853, when the first [plats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plat) for the village were filed. In 1855, nominal land settlements were established. On January 14, 1865, the Legislature of Territorial Washington incorporated the Town of Seattle with a [board of trustees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trustee_model_of_representation) managing the city. The Town of Seattle was disincorporated on January 18, 1867, and remained a mere precinct of King County until late 1869, when a new petition was filed and the city was re-incorporated December 2, 1869, with a [mayor–council government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government).[^cite_note-founding-31][^cite_note-40] The corporate seal of the City of Seattle carries the date "1869" and a likeness of Chief Seattle in left profile.[^cite_note-41] That same year, Seattle acquired the epithet of the "Queen City", a designation officially changed in 1982 to the "Emerald City".[^cite_note-42]

Seattle has a history of boom-and-bust cycles, like many other cities near areas of extensive natural and mineral resources. Seattle has risen several times economically, then gone into precipitous decline, but it has typically used those periods to rebuild solid infrastructure.[^cite_note-shear-43]

The first such boom, covering the early years of the city, rode on the lumber industry. During this period the road now known as [Yesler Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesler_Way) won the nickname "Skid Road", supposedly after the timber skidding down the hill to [Henry Yesler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Yesler)'s sawmill. The later dereliction of the area may be a possible origin for the term which later entered the wider American lexicon as *[Skid Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_Row)*.[^cite_note-yesler-44] Like much of the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States), Seattle experienced conflicts between labor and management and ethnic tensions that culminated in the [anti-Chinese riots of 1885–1886](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Seattle_before_1900#Relations_between_whites_and_Chinese).[^cite_note-Kinnear_1911-45] This violence originated with unemployed whites who were determined to drive the Chinese from Seattle; anti-Chinese riots also occurred in [Tacoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tacoma_riot_of_1885).

Seattle had achieved sufficient economic success when the [Great Seattle Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Seattle_Fire) of 1889 destroyed the central business district. However, a far grander city center rapidly emerged in its place.[^cite_note-fire-46] Finance company [Washington Mutual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Mutual), for example, was founded in the immediate wake of the fire.[^cite_note-Klondike-foundings-47] The [Panic of 1893](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic_of_1893) hit Seattle hard.[^cite_note-48]

The second and most dramatic boom resulted from the [Klondike Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klondike_Gold_Rush), which ended the depression that had begun with the [Panic of 1893](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic_of_1893). In a short time, Seattle became a major transportation center. On July 14, 1897, the *S.S. Portland* docked with its famed "ton of gold", and Seattle became the main transport and supply point for the miners in [Alaska](624.md) and the [Yukon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukon). Few of those working men found lasting wealth. However, it was Seattle's business of clothing the miners and feeding them salmon that panned out in the long run. Along with Seattle, other cities like [Everett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everett,_Washington), [Tacoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tacoma,_Washington), [Port Townsend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Townsend,_Washington), [Bremerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bremerton,_Washington), and [Olympia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympia,_Washington), all in the Puget Sound region, became competitors for exchange, rather than [mother lodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_lode) for extraction, of precious metals.[^cite_note-49]



A September 1870 engraving of Seattle published in *[Harper's Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harper's_Magazine)*

### 20th century



[Pioneer Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Square,_Seattle), the [Pioneer Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Building_\(Seattle,_Washington\)), the [Smith Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smith_Tower), and the [Seattle Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Hotel) in 1917



The [Seattle Center Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Center_Monorail) under construction in 1961

The boom lasted into the early part of the 20th century, and funded many new Seattle companies and products. In 1907, 19-year-old [James E. Casey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_E._Casey) borrowed \$100 from a friend and founded the [American Messenger Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Messenger_Company) (later [UPS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Parcel_Service)). Other Seattle companies founded during this period include [Nordstrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordstrom) and [Eddie Bauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Bauer).[^cite_note-Klondike-foundings-47] Seattle brought in the [Olmsted Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olmsted_Brothers) landscape architecture firm to design a system of parks and boulevards.[^cite_note-50] The Gold Rush era culminated in the [Alaska–Yukon–Pacific Exposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska%E2%80%93Yukon%E2%80%93Pacific_Exposition) of 1909, which is largely responsible for the layout of today's [University of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Washington) campus.[^cite_note-aypexpo-51]

A [shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipbuilding) boom in the early part of the 20th century became massive during [World War I](4764461.md), making Seattle somewhat of a company town. The subsequent retrenchment led to the [Seattle General Strike of 1919](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_General_Strike), an early [general strike](41138909.md) in the country.[^cite_note-generalstrike-52] A 1912 city development plan by [Virgil Bogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgil_Bogue) went largely unused. Seattle was mildly prosperous in the 1920s but was particularly hard hit in the Great Depression, experiencing some of the country's harshest labor strife in that era. Violence during the [Maritime Strike of 1934](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Strike_of_1934) cost Seattle much of its maritime traffic, which was rerouted to the [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-53]

Seattle was one of the major cities that benefited from programs such as the [Works Progress Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Works_Progress_Administration), [CCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilian_Conservation_Corps), [Public Works Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Works_Administration), and others.[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55] The workers, mostly men, built roads, parks, dams, schools, railroads, bridges, docks, and even historical and archival record sites and buildings. Seattle faced significant unemployment, loss of lumber and construction industries as [Los Angeles](18110.md) prevailed as the bigger [West Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_of_the_United_States) city. Seattle had building contracts that rivaled [New York City](645042.md) and [Chicago](6886.md), but also lost to Los Angeles. Seattle's eastern farm land faded due to [Oregon](26811621.md)'s and the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States)'s, forcing people into town.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57]

[Hooverville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooverville) arose during the Depression, leading to Seattle's growing homeless population. Stationed outside Seattle, the Hooverville housed thousands of men but very few children and no women. With work projects close to the city, Hooverville grew and the WPA settled into the city.[^cite_note-58]

A movement of women arose from Seattle during the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression), fueled in part by [Eleanor Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleanor_Roosevelt)'s 1933 book *It's Up to the Women*; women pushed for recognition, not just as housewives, but as the backbone of the family. Using newspapers and journals *Working Woman* and *The Woman Today*, women pushed to be seen as equal and receive some recognition.[^cite_note-59]

The Great Depression did not impact the [University of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Washington) negatively. As schools across Washington lost funding and attendance, the university actually prospered during the time period as they focused on growing their student enrollment. While [Seattle public schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Public_Schools) were influenced by Washington's superintendent Worth McClure,[^cite_note-60] they still struggled to pay teachers and maintain attendance.[^cite_note-61]

Seattle was the home base of impresario [Alexander Pantages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Pantages) who, starting in 1902, opened a number of theaters in the city exhibiting [vaudeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaudeville) acts and silent movies. He went on to become one of America's greatest theater and movie tycoons. Scottish-born architect [B. Marcus Priteca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._Marcus_Priteca) designed several theaters for Pantages in Seattle, which were later demolished or converted to other uses. Seattle's surviving [Paramount Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Theatre_\(Seattle\)), on which he collaborated, was not a Pantages theater.[^cite_note-62]

War work again brought local prosperity during [World War II](32927.md), centered on the production of [Boeing](18933266.md) aircraft. The war dispersed the city's numerous Japanese-American businessmen due to the [Japanese American internment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_American_internment). After the World War II, however, the local economy dipped. It rose again with Boeing's growing dominance in the commercial [airliner](51215.md) market.[^cite_note-63] Seattle celebrated its restored prosperity and made a bid for world recognition with the [Century 21 Exposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Century_21_Exposition), the [1962 World's Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Century_21_Exposition), for which the [Space Needle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Needle) was built.[^cite_note-worldsfair-64]

Another major local economic downturn was in the late 1960s and early 1970s, at a time when Boeing was heavily affected by the [oil crises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970s_energy_crisis), loss of government contracts, and costs and delays associated with the [Boeing 747](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_747). Many people left the area to look for work elsewhere, and two local real estate agents put up a billboard reading "Will the last person leaving Seattle – Turn out the lights."[^cite_note-65]



747 prototype "City of Everett" at Museum of Flight in Seattle.

Seattle remained the corporate headquarters of Boeing until 2001, when the company separated its headquarters from its major production facilities; the headquarters were moved to Chicago.[^cite_note-66] The Seattle area is still home to Boeing's [Renton narrow-body plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Renton_Factory) and [Everett wide-body plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Everett_Factory).[^cite_note-67] The company's [credit union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Credit_union) for employees, [BECU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BECU), remains based in the Seattle area and has been open to all residents of Washington since 2002.[^cite_note-68]

On March 20, 1970, twenty-eight people were killed when the Ozark Hotel was burned by an unknown arsonist.[^cite_note-69] The [Wah Mee massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wah_Mee_massacre) in 1983 resulted in the killing of 13 people in an illegal gambling club in the [Seattle Chinatown-International District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Chinatown-International_District).[^cite_note-70]

The city's first [coffeehouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_in_Seattle) were established in the late 1950s and grew over the following decades as espresso was introduced to the American market. On March 30, 1971, the [first location](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Starbucks) for [Starbucks Coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) opened at Pike Place Market in Downtown Seattle. The company initially sold [coffee beans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_bean) but later expanded into cafes.[^cite_note-71]

Prosperity began to return in the 1980s beginning with [Microsoft](19001.md)'s 1979 move from [Albuquerque, New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque,_New_Mexico), to nearby [Bellevue, Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellevue,_Washington).[^cite_note-72]

Seattle and its suburbs became home to a number of technology companies, including [Amazon](90451.md), [F5 Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F5_Networks), [RealNetworks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RealNetworks), [Nintendo of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo_of_America), and [T-Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Mobile_US).[^cite_note-73] This success brought an influx of new residents with a population increase within city limits of almost 50,000 between 1990 and 2000,[^cite_note-pophistory-74] and saw Seattle's real estate become some of the most expensive in the country.[^cite_note-75]

Seattle in this period attracted attention as home to the companies opened operations in or around the city. In 1990, the [Goodwill Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwill_Games) were held in the city.[^cite_note-goodwillgames-76] Three years later, in 1993, the [APEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APEC) leaders was hosted in Seattle.[^cite_note-77] The 1990s also witnessed a growing popularity in [grunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grunge) music, a sound that was largely developed in Seattle's independent music scene.[^cite_note-78]

In 1993, the movie *[Sleepless in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleepless_in_Seattle)* brought the city further national attention,[^cite_note-79] as did the television sitcom *[Frasier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frasier)*. The [dot-com boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dot-com_bubble) caused a great frenzy among the technology companies in Seattle but the bubble ended in early 2001.[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81]

In 1999, the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization_Ministerial_Conference_of_1999) held its conference in Seattle, which was met with [protest activity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization_Ministerial_Conference_of_1999_protest_activity). The protests and police reactions to them largely overshadowed the conference itself.[^cite_note-wto-82]

### 21st century

In 2001, the city was impacted by the [Mardi Gras Riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Mardi_Gras_Riots) and then by the [Nisqually earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Nisqually_earthquake) the following day.[^cite_note-83]

Another boom began as the city emerged from the [Great Recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession), commencing when [Amazon](90451.md) moved its headquarters from North [Beacon Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beacon_Hill,_Seattle) to [South Lake Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lake_Union,_Seattle). The move initiated a historic construction boom which resulted in the completion of almost 10,000 apartments in Seattle in 2017, more than any previous year and nearly twice as many as were built in 2016.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-Rosenberg-85] By 2025, Seattle's new apartments had become the smallest in the U.S., with an average of 649 square feet (60.3 m<sup>2</sup>) among all unit types.[^cite_note-86]

From 2010 to 2015, Seattle gained an average of 14,511 residents per year, with the growth strongly skewed toward the center of the city,[^cite_note-87] and unemployment dropped from roughly 9 percent to 3.6 percent.[^cite_note-pi-slu-boom-88] The city has found itself "bursting at the seams", with over 45,000 households spending more than half their income on housing and [at least 2,800 people homeless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_Seattle), and with the country's sixth-worst rush-hour traffic.[^cite_note-pi-slu-boom-88] In response, many [urbanist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanism) measures to improve transit, cycling,[^cite_note-auto-89] and housing affordability were passed by city council, the state, and voters.[^cite_note-90] These include [Sound Transit 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_Transit_3), Seattle Prop 1A,[^cite_note-91] a new "One Seattle" Comprehensive Plan,[^cite_note-92] and electing [Katie Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Wilson) to mayor.

In 2020, protesters against the [murder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd) of [George Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd) and police over-funding established a [self-declared autonomous zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill_Occupied_Protest) in the [Capitol Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill,_Seattle) neighborhood of Seattle.[^cite_note-93]

During the early to mid 2020s, large numbers of transgender people fled to Seattle from conservative-leaning states, which had implemented [hostile legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020s_anti-LGBTQ_movement_in_the_United_States) restricting their civil rights. This led the city's LGBTQ commission to send a letter to the mayor recommending a state of civil emergency be declared.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96]

## Geography

### Topography

See also: [Bodies of water of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodies_of_water_of_Seattle), [List of neighborhoods in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_neighborhoods_in_Seattle), and [Regrading in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regrading_in_Seattle)



A satellite photo of Seattle in September 2018

Seattle is located between the saltwater [Puget Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puget_Sound) (an arm of the Pacific Ocean) to the west and [Lake Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Washington) to the east. The city's chief harbor, [Elliott Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Bay), is part of Puget Sound, making the city an oceanic port. To the west, beyond Puget Sound, are the [Kitsap Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitsap_Peninsula) and [Olympic Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Mountains) on the [Olympic Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Peninsula); to the east, beyond Lake Washington and the [Eastside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastside_\(King_County,_Washington\)) suburbs, are [Lake Sammamish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Sammamish) and the [Cascade Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascade_Range). Lake Washington's waters flow to Puget Sound through the [Lake Washington Ship Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Washington_Ship_Canal) (consisting of two man-made canals, [Lake Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Union), and the [Hiram M. Chittenden Locks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiram_M._Chittenden_Locks) at [Salmon Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmon_Bay), ending in [Shilshole Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shilshole_Bay) on Puget Sound).

The sea, rivers, forests, lakes, and fields surrounding Seattle were once rich enough to support one of the world's few sedentary [hunter-gatherer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) societies. In modern times the surrounding area lends itself well to sailing, skiing, bicycling, camping, and hiking year-round.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98]

The city is hilly in some places.[^cite_note-99] Like Rome, the city is said to lie on [seven hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_hills_of_Seattle);[^cite_note-100] the lists vary but typically include [Capitol Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill,_Seattle), [First Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Hill,_Seattle), [West Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Seattle), [Beacon Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beacon_Hill,_Seattle), [Queen Anne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Anne,_Seattle), Magnolia, and the former [Denny Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denny_Hill). The [Wallingford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallingford,_Seattle), [Delridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delridge,_Seattle), [Mount Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Baker,_Seattle), [Seward Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seward_Park,_Seattle), [Washington Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Park,_Seattle), [Broadmoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadmoor,_Seattle), [Madrona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrona,_Seattle), [Phinney Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phinney_Ridge,_Seattle), [Sunset Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_Hill,_Seattle), [Blue Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Ridge,_Seattle), [Broadview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadview,_Seattle), [Laurelhurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurelhurst,_Seattle), [Hawthorne Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawthorne_Hills,_Seattle), [Maple Leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_Leaf,_Seattle), and [Crown Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Hill,_Seattle) neighborhoods are all located on hills. Many of the hilliest areas are near the city center, with Capitol Hill, First Hill, and Beacon Hill collectively constituting something of a ridge along an isthmus between Elliott Bay and Lake Washington.[^cite_note-101] The break in the ridge between First Hill and Beacon Hill is man-made, a result of two of the many [regrading projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regrading_in_Seattle) that reshaped the topography of the city center.[^cite_note-102] The topography of the city center was also changed by the construction of a [seawall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seawall) and the artificial [Harbor Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbor_Island,_Seattle) (completed 1909) at the mouth of the city's industrial [Duwamish Waterway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duwamish_Waterway), the terminus of the [Green River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_River_\(Duwamish_River_tributary\)). The highest point within city limits, 520 feet (160 m) above sea level, is at Myrtle Reservoir Park in the [High Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Point,_Seattle) neighborhood of West Seattle.[^cite_note-Times-Hill-103]

North of the city center, the Lake Washington Ship Canal connects Puget Sound to Lake Washington. It incorporates four natural bodies of water: [Lake Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Union), [Salmon Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmon_Bay), [Portage Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portage_Bay), and [Union Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Bay_\(Seattle\)).[^cite_note-104]

Due to its location in the [Pacific Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Ring_of_Fire), Seattle is in a major [earthquake zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seismic_zone). On February 28, 2001, the [magnitude 6.8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richter_magnitude_scale) [Nisqually earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Nisqually_earthquake) did significant architectural damage, especially in the [Pioneer Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Square,_Seattle) area (built on [reclaimed land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation), as are the [Industrial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_District,_Seattle) and part of the city center), and caused one fatality.[^cite_note-105] Other strong earthquakes occurred on [January 26, 1700](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1700_Cascadia_earthquake) (estimated at 9 magnitude), [December 14, 1872](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1872_North_Cascades_earthquake) (7.3 or 7.4),[^cite_note-106] [April 13, 1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Olympia_earthquake) (7.1),[^cite_note-1949-quake-107] and April 29, 1965 (6.5).[^cite_note-1965-quake-108] The [1965 quake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965_Puget_Sound_earthquake) caused three deaths in Seattle directly and one more by heart failure.[^cite_note-1965-quake-108] Although the [Seattle Fault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Fault) passes just south of the city center, neither it[^cite_note-109] nor the [Cascadia subduction zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascadia_subduction_zone) has caused an earthquake since the city's founding. The Cascadia subduction zone poses the threat of an earthquake of magnitude 9.0 or greater, capable of seriously damaging the city and collapsing many buildings, especially in zones built on fill.[^cite_note-110]

According to the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau), the city has a total area of 142.5 square miles (369 km<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-GR1-111] 84 square miles (220 km<sup>2</sup>) of which is land and 58.1 square miles (150 km<sup>2</sup>) is water (41% of the total area).[^cite_note-CenPopGazetteer-2]

### Cityscape

Further information: [List of tallest buildings in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Seattle) and [Architecture of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Seattle)



A Seattle skyline view from Queen Anne Hill, including the [Space Needle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Needle), [Climate Pledge Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_Pledge_Arena), [Mount Rainier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Rainier), [Elliott Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Bay), and the Port of Seattle on [Puget Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puget_Sound)

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Seattle)

According to the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) system, Seattle has a warm-summer [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate) (*Csb*),[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114] while under the [Trewartha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trewartha_climate_classification) system, it is labeled an [oceanic climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) (*Dobk*).[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] It has cool, wet winters and mild, relatively dry summers, covering characteristics of both climate types.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] The climate is sometimes characterized as a "modified Mediterranean" climate because it is cooler and wetter than a "true" Mediterranean climate, but shares the characteristic dry summer (which has a strong influence on the region's vegetation).[^cite_note-GobleHirt2012-119]

Seattle's strong seasonal contrast reflects the migration of the dominant North Pacific pressure centers. In summer, the [North Pacific High](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Pacific_High) expands northward over the eastern Pacific and deflects the storm track far to the north of Washington.[^cite_note-WRCC_WA_seattle-120] From late autumn through early spring the high retreats southward while the [Aleutian Low](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Low), a semi-permanent low-pressure system near the [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands), intensifies and shifts south, becoming one of the dominant features of Northern Hemisphere atmospheric circulation.[^cite_note-WRCC_WA_seattle-120][^cite_note-Rodionov2007_seattle-121] Extratropical cyclones forming in the sub-polar North Pacific typically reach peak intensity near the Aleutian Low and are then steered by the polar [jet stream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jet_stream) toward the Pacific Northwest coast, delivering the great majority of Seattle's annual precipitation between October and March.[^cite_note-Rodionov2007_seattle-121] Particularly intense storms include [atmospheric rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atmospheric_river) ("[Pineapple Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pineapple_Express)" systems) that channel subtropical moisture into western Washington and can produce four to five inches of rainfall in twenty-four hours.[^cite_note-Dettinger2011_seattle-122]

The city's precipitation is further shaped by its position in the lee of the [Olympic Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Mountains), which rise abruptly to nearly 8,000 feet (2,400 m) only about 60 miles (97 km) to the west and force [orographic uplift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orographic_lift) of incoming Pacific air. The windward [Hoh Rainforest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoh_Rainforest) averages roughly 142 inches (3,600 mm) of precipitation annually, while the [rain shadow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_shadow) on the leeward side produces only about 16 inches (410 mm) at [Sequim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequim,_Washington) in the Dungeness Valley, one of the steepest precipitation gradients in the contiguous United States.[^cite_note-NPS_Olympic_seattle-123][^cite_note-rainshadow-124] Seattle sits near the eastern edge of this shadow and is only partially sheltered by it.

Temperature extremes are moderated by the adjacent [Puget Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puget_Sound), greater [Pacific Ocean](23070.md), and [Lake Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Washington). Thus extreme [heat waves](293270.md) are rare in the Seattle area, as are very cold temperatures (below about 15 °F; −9 °C). The Seattle area is the cloudiest region of the [Continental United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_United_States), with 201 cloudy days and 93 partly cloudy days in an average year.[^cite_note-cloudy-125] With many more "rain days" than other major American cities, Seattle has a well-earned reputation for frequent rain.[^cite_note-rainshadow-124] In an average year, there are 150 days in which at least 0.01 inches (0.25 mm) of precipitation falls, more days than in nearly all U.S. cities east of the [Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountains).[^cite_note-126] However, because it often has merely a light [drizzle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drizzle) falling from the sky for many days, Seattle actually receives significantly less rainfall (or other precipitation) overall than many other major U.S. cities like [New York City](645042.md), [Miami](53846.md), or [Houston](13774.md).

The most damaging weather events in the region are deep extratropical cyclones spawned along the Aleutian Low storm track, which periodically produce some of the strongest non-tropical windstorms in the contiguous United States.[^cite_note-MassDotson2010_seattle-127] The benchmark event is the [Columbus Day storm of 1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus_Day_storm_of_1962), which brought sustained winds above 100 miles per hour (160 km/h) to western Washington and killed 46 people across the region; the [Hanukkah Eve Wind Storm of 2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanukkah_Eve_Wind_Storm_of_2006) cut power to approximately 1.8 million customers and caused damages exceeding \$1 billion.[^cite_note-MyNW_Storms_seattle-128]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Seattle)

In the 2012–2016 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey) (ACS), the racial makeup of the city was 65.7% [White Non-Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Non-Hispanic), 16.9% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American), 6.8% [Black or African American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American), 6.6% [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanics_in_the_United_States) or [Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) of any race, 0.4% [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States), 0.9% [Pacific Islander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_American), 0.2% other races, and 5.6% [two or more races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_American).[^cite_note-135]

Seattle's population historically has been predominantly white.[^cite_note-pop-140] The 2010 census showed that Seattle was one of the [whitest big cities in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_U.S._municipalities_by_race/ethnicity_in_2020), although its proportion of white residents has been gradually declining.[^cite_note-143] In 1960, whites constituted 91.6% of the city's population,[^cite_note-pop-140] while in 2010 they constituted 69.5%.[^cite_note-factfinder2.census.gov-144][^cite_note-c2010-145] According to the 2006–2008 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey), approximately 78.9% of residents over the age of five spoke only English at home. Those who spoke [Asian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Asia) other than Indo-European languages made up 10.2% of the population, Spanish was spoken by 4.5% of the population, speakers of other [Indo-European languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_languages) made up 3.9%, and speakers of other languages made up 2.5%.



Ethnic origins in Seattle



A racial distribution map of Seattle, 2010 U.S. Census. Each dot is 25 people: ⬤ White ⬤ Black ⬤ Asian ⬤ Hispanic ⬤ Other

Seattle's foreign-born population grew 40% between the 1990 and 2000 censuses.[^cite_note-brookings-146] The [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_American) population in the Seattle area has origins in [mainland China](55079.md), Hong Kong, Southeast Asia, and [Taiwan](25734.md). The earliest Chinese-Americans that came in the late 19th and early 20th centuries were almost entirely from [Guangdong Province](65109.md). In 2007, the Seattle area was also home to a large [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_American) population of more than 55,000 residents.[^cite_note-147] In 2013, there were over 30,000 [Somali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somali_American) immigrants.[^cite_note-Lsts-148]

In 2004, the Seattle-Tacoma area was home to one of the largest [Cambodian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian_American) communities in the United States, numbering about 19,000 Cambodian Americans.[^cite_note-Turnbull2004-149] In 2010, Seattle had one of the largest [Samoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_American) communities in the mainland U.S., with over 15,000 people having Samoan ancestry.[^cite_note-factfinder2.census.gov-144][^cite_note-pugetsoundsamoan-150] In 2000, Seattle area had the highest percentage of self-identified mixed-race people of any large metropolitan area in the United States.[^cite_note-151] In 2012, Seattle's 98118 ZIP code, in the Columbia City neighborhood, was one of the most diverse ZIP Code Tabulation Areas in the United States.[^cite_note-zipcode-152]

In 2018, the median income of a Seattle household was \$93,481, and the median income for a family was \$130,656.[^cite_note-income-153] 11.0% of the population and 6.6% of families were below the poverty line. Of people living in poverty, 11.4% were under the age of 18 and 10.9% were 65 or older.[^cite_note-income-153]

In 2007, King County had an estimated 8,000 homeless people on any given night, and many of those live in Seattle.[^cite_note-154] In September 2005, King County adopted a "Ten-Year Plan to End Homelessness", one of the near-term results of which is a shift of funding from [homeless shelter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeless_shelter) beds to permanent housing.[^cite_note-homelessness-155]

In recent years, the city has experienced steady population growth, and has been faced with the issue of accommodating more residents. In 2006, after growing by 4,000 citizens per year for the previous 16 years, regional planners expected the population of Seattle to grow by 200,000 people by 2040.[^cite_note-Young2006-156] Former mayor Greg Nickels supported plans that would increase the population by 60%, or 350,000 people, by 2040 and worked on ways to accommodate this growth while keeping Seattle's single-family housing zoning laws.[^cite_note-Young2006-156] The Seattle City Council later voted to relax height limits on buildings in the greater part of Downtown, partly with the aim to increase residential density in the city center.[^cite_note-157] As a sign of increasing downtown core growth, the [Downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Seattle) population crested to over 60,000 in 2009, up 77% since 1990.[^cite_note-158]

In 2020, Seattle was one of the first US cities affected by COVID-19.[^cite_note-159] At the same time, Seattle experienced its first population decline in 50 years.[^cite_note-160] This was followed by five straight years of population growth that exceeded 2% per year. Seattle surpassed 800,000 residents in 2025.[^cite_note-161]

Seattle has a relatively high number of adults living alone. In 2004, Seattle had the fifth highest proportion of single-person households nationwide among cities of 100,000 or more residents, at 40.8%.[^cite_note-162]

### Sexual orientation and gender identity

See also: [History of the LGBTQ community in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_LGBTQ_community_in_Seattle), [LGBTQ culture in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_culture_in_Seattle), and [Seattle Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Pride)



Seattle Pride Fest 2024

Seattle has a notably large [lesbian, gay, bisexual, and transgender](78174325.md) community. In 2006, 12.9% of city residents polled identified as gay, lesbian, or bisexual. This was the second-highest proportion of any major U.S. city, behind [San Francisco](49728.md).[^cite_note-gates_06-163] Greater Seattle also ranked second among major U.S. metropolitan areas, with 6.5% of the population identifying as gay, lesbian, or bisexual.[^cite_note-gates_06-163]

In 2015, Seattle ranked fifth, at 4.8%.[^cite_note-Fifth2015-19] In 2012, Seattle has the highest percentage of same-sex households in the United States, at 2.6 percent, surpassing San Francisco's 2.5 percent.[^cite_note-164] The [Capitol Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill,_Seattle) district has been the center of LGBTQ culture in Seattle[^cite_note-165] since the 1970s. Before then, the [Pioneer Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Square,_Seattle) district was the city's hub of LGBTQ community.[^cite_note-Freitas2017-166]

## Economy

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(April 2021)* |
| --- | --- |

See also: [List of companies based in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_based_in_Seattle)



[Washington Mutual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Mutual)'s last headquarters, the WaMu Center, now the [Russell Investments Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Investments_Center), (center left), and its prior headquarters, Washington Mutual Tower, now the [1201 Third Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1201_Third_Avenue) Tower



The corporate headquarters of online retailer [Amazon](90451.md), named Day 1 and located in Denny Triangle

Seattle's economy is driven by a mix of older industrial companies and new-economy internet and technology companies, as well as service, design, and [clean technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_technology) companies. The city's gross metropolitan product (GMP) was \$231 billion in 2010, making it the [11th-largest metropolitan economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_in_the_United_States_by_GMP) in the United States.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] The [Port of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Seattle), which also operates [Seattle–Tacoma International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle%E2%80%93Tacoma_International_Airport), is a major gateway for trade with Asia and cruises to Alaska. It also is the 8th-largest port in the United States when measured by container capacity. Its maritime cargo operations merged with the [Port of Tacoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Tacoma) in 2015 to form the [Northwest Seaport Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Seaport_Alliance).[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170]

Although it was impacted by the [Great Recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession_in_the_United_States), Seattle has retained a comparatively strong economy, and is noted for start-up businesses, especially in green building and clean technologies.[^cite_note-171] In February 2010, the city government committed Seattle to become North America's first "climate neutral" city, with a goal of reaching net-zero per-capita greenhouse gas emissions by 2030.[^cite_note-172]

Large companies continue to dominate the business landscape. Seven companies on [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500)'s 2022 list of the United States' largest companies (based on total revenue) are headquartered in Seattle: Internet retailer [Amazon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon.com) (#2), coffee chain [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) (#120), freight forwarder [Expeditors International of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expeditors_International_of_Washington) (#225), department store [Nordstrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordstrom) (#245), forest products company [Weyerhaeuser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weyerhaeuser) (#354), online travel company [Expedia Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expedia_Group) (#404), and real-estate tech company [Zillow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zillow) (#424) .[^cite_note-Fortune500-173] Other Fortune 500 companies commonly associated with Seattle are based in nearby Puget Sound cities. Warehouse club chain [Costco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costco) (#11), the largest retail company in Washington, is based in [Issaquah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issaquah,_Washington). [Microsoft](19001.md) (#14) is located in [Redmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redmond,_Washington). Furthermore, Bellevue is home to truck manufacturer [Paccar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paccar) (#151).[^cite_note-Fortune500-173] Other major companies headquartered in the area include [Nintendo of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo_of_America) in Redmond, [T-Mobile US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Mobile_US) in Bellevue, and [Providence Health & Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence_Health_&_Services) (the state's largest health care system and fifth-largest employer) in [Renton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renton,_Washington). The city has a reputation for heavy [coffee consumption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_in_Seattle),[^cite_note-nyt-coffee-174] with a landscape featuring Seattle-based companies such as Starbucks,[^cite_note-175] [Seattle's Best Coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle's_Best_Coffee),[^cite_note-176] and [Tully's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tully's_Coffee)[^cite_note-177] alongside numerous independent artisanal espresso roasters and cafés.[^cite_note-nyt-coffee-174][^cite_note-178]

Before moving its headquarters to [Chicago](6886.md) and then ultimately [Arlington County, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia), aerospace manufacturer [Boeing](18933266.md) (#60) was the largest company based in Seattle. Its largest division, [Boeing Commercial Airplanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_Commercial_Airplanes), is still headquartered within the Puget Sound region.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] The company also has large aircraft manufacturing plants in Everett and Renton; it remains the largest private employer in the Seattle metropolitan area.[^cite_note-181] In 2006 former Seattle Mayor Greg Nickels announced a desire to spark a new economic boom driven by the [biotechnology](4502.md) industry. Major redevelopment of the [South Lake Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lake_Union,_Seattle) neighborhood is underway in an effort to attract new and established biotech companies to the city, joining biotech companies [Corixa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corixa_\(company\)) (acquired by [GlaxoSmithKline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GlaxoSmithKline)), Immunex (now part of Amgen), [Trubion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trubion), and ZymoGenetics. [Vulcan Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulcan_Inc.), the holding company of billionaire [Paul Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Allen), is behind most of the development projects in the region. While some see the new development as an economic boon, others have criticized Nickels and the [Seattle City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_City_Council) for pandering to Allen's interests at taxpayers' expense.[^cite_note-182]

In 2005, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* ranked Seattle as the most expensive American city for buying a house based on the local income levels.[^cite_note-Clemence-183] In response to advocacy concerning the rapidly increasing [cost of living](522934.md) at the time, Seattle passed an [ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle's_minimum_wage_ordinance) in 2014 to gradually increase the [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage) to [\$15 per hour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\$15_per_hour) by 2017 and increase it annually thereafter according to the metropolitan area's [consumer price index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index) (CPI).[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-2025_wage_-_Times-185] To address concerns from business proprietors, the ordinance included carveouts for [small businesses](276495.md) and [gratuity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gratuity) that were phased out at the end of 2024.[^cite_note-2025_wage_-_Times-185][^cite_note-186] Seattle and its metropolitan area currently have one of the [highest minimum wages in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage_in_the_United_States) at more than \$20 per hour as of 2025.[^cite_note-2025_wage_-_Times-185]

Operating a hub at Seattle–Tacoma International Airport, [Alaska Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Airlines) maintains its headquarters in the city of [SeaTac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeaTac,_Washington), next to the airport.[^cite_note-187] Seattle is a hub for global health with the headquarters of the [Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_&_Melinda_Gates_Foundation), [PATH (global health organization)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PATH_\(global_health_organization\)), [Infectious Disease Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infectious_Disease_Research_Institute), [Fred Hutchinson Cancer Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Hutchinson_Cancer_Center), and the [Institute for Health Metrics and Evaluation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Health_Metrics_and_Evaluation). In 2015, the Washington Global Health Alliance counted 168 global health organizations in Washington state. Many are headquartered in Seattle.[^cite_note-188]

## Culture

Many of [Seattle's neighborhoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_neighborhoods_in_Seattle) host one or more [street fairs or parades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Seattle_street_fairs_and_parades).[^cite_note-189]

### Performing arts

Main articles: [Arts in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arts_in_Seattle) and [Music of Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Seattle)

See also: [List of musicians from Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_musicians_from_Seattle) and [List of songs about Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_songs_about_Seattle)



[Kreielsheimer Promenade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kreielsheimer_Promenade) and [Marion Oliver McCaw Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_Oliver_McCaw_Hall) at [Seattle Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Center)



[Benaroya Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benaroya_Hall), home of the [Seattle Symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Symphony) since 1998

Seattle has been a regional center for the [performing arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performing_arts) for many years. The century-old [Seattle Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Symphony_Orchestra) has won many awards and performs primarily at [Benaroya Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benaroya_Hall).[^cite_note-190] The [Seattle Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Opera) and [Pacific Northwest Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Northwest_Ballet), which perform at [McCaw Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCaw_Hall) (opened in 2003 on the site of the former Seattle Opera House at Seattle Center), are comparably distinguished,[^cite_note-pnb-191][^cite_note-192] with the Opera being particularly known for its performances of the works of [Richard Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner)[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] and the PNB School (founded in 1974) ranking as one of the top three ballet training institutions in the United States.[^cite_note-195] The [Seattle Youth Symphony Orchestras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Youth_Symphony_Orchestras) (SYSO) is the largest symphonic youth organization in the United States.[^cite_note-196] The city also boasts lauded summer and winter [chamber music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_music) festivals organized by the [Seattle Chamber Music Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Chamber_Music_Society).[^cite_note-197]

The [5th Avenue Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5th_Avenue_Theatre), built in 1926, stages [Broadway-style](725252.md) musical shows[^cite_note-198] featuring both local talent and international stars.[^cite_note-199] Seattle has "around 100" theatrical production companies[^cite_note-Kiley-new-theater-200] and over two dozen live theatre venues, many of them associated with [fringe theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fringe_theatre);[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] Seattle is probably second only to New York for number of [equity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actors'_Equity_Association) theaters[^cite_note-203] (28 Seattle theater companies have some sort of [Actors' Equity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actors'_Equity) contract).[^cite_note-Kiley-new-theater-200] In addition, the 900-seat [Romanesque Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_Revival_architecture) [Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_Hall_Seattle) on First Hill hosts numerous cultural events, especially lectures and recitals.[^cite_note-204]

Between 1918 and 1951, there were nearly two dozen jazz nightclubs along Jackson Street, running from the current Chinatown/International District to the Central District. The jazz scene developed the early careers of [Ray Charles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Charles), [Quincy Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Jones), [Bumps Blackwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bumps_Blackwell), [Ernestine Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernestine_Anderson), and others.[^cite_note-Seattle_Music-205]

Early popular musical acts from the Seattle/Puget Sound area include the collegiate folk group [The Brothers Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Brothers_Four), vocal group [The Fleetwoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fleetwoods), 1960s garage rockers [The Wailers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wailers_\(rock_band\)) and [The Sonics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sonics), and instrumental surf group [The Ventures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ventures), some of whom are still active.[^cite_note-Seattle_Music-205]

Seattle is considered the home of [grunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grunge) music,[^cite_note-Seattle_Sound-20] having produced artists such as [Nirvana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nirvana_\(band\)), [Soundgarden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundgarden), [Alice in Chains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_in_Chains), [Pearl Jam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Jam), and [Mudhoney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mudhoney), all of whom reached international audiences in the early 1990s.[^cite_note-Seattle_Music-205] The city is also home to such varied artists as [avant-garde jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant-garde_jazz) musicians [Bill Frisell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Frisell) and [Wayne Horvitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_Horvitz), [hot jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_music) musician [Glenn Crytzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Crytzer), [hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_hop_music) artists [Sir Mix-a-Lot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Mix-a-Lot), [Macklemore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macklemore), [Blue Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Scholars), and [Shabazz Palaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabazz_Palaces), [smooth jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smooth_jazz) saxophonist [Kenny G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenny_G), [classic rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_rock) staples [Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_\(band\)) and [Queensrÿche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queensr%C3%BFche), and alternative rock bands such as [Foo Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foo_Fighters), [Harvey Danger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvey_Danger), [The Presidents of the United States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Presidents_of_the_United_States_of_America_\(band\)), [The Posies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Posies), [Modest Mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modest_Mouse), [Band of Horses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band_of_Horses), [Death Cab for Cutie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_Cab_for_Cutie), and [Fleet Foxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleet_Foxes). Rock musicians such as [Jimi Hendrix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimi_Hendrix), [Duff McKagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duff_McKagan), and [Nikki Sixx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Sixx) spent their formative years in Seattle.

The Seattle-based [Sub Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub_Pop) record company continues to be one of the world's best-known independent/alternative music labels.[^cite_note-Seattle_Music-205] Seattle is known for its live-music venues including [The Crocodile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Crocodile), [Vito's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vito's) and Columbia City Theater.[^cite_note-206] Over the years, [a number of songs have been written about Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_songs_about_Seattle).

Seattle annually sends a team of [spoken word](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoken_word) slammers to the [National Poetry Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Poetry_Slam) and considers itself home to such performance poets as [Buddy Wakefield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddy_Wakefield), two-time [Individual World Poetry Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_World_Poetry_Slam) Champ;[^cite_note-207] [Anis Mojgani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anis_Mojgani), two-time National Poetry Slam Champ;[^cite_note-208] and [Danny Sherrard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Sherrard), 2007 National Poetry Slam Champ and 2008 Individual World Poetry Slam Champ.[^cite_note-209] Seattle also hosted the 2001 national Poetry Slam Tournament. The Seattle Poetry Festival is a biennial poetry festival that (launched first as the Poetry Circus in 1997) has featured local, regional, national, and international names in poetry.[^cite_note-210]

The city also has movie houses showing both [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles,_California) productions and works by [independent filmmakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_film).[^cite_note-211] Among these, the [Seattle Cinerama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Cinerama) stands out as one of only three movie theaters in the world still capable of showing three-panel [Cinerama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinerama) films.[^cite_note-212]

### Tourism

See also: [List of museums in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Seattle)



210 cruise ship visits brought 886,039 passengers to Seattle in 2008.

[^cite_note-213]



The [Seattle Great Wheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Great_Wheel)

Among Seattle's prominent annual fairs and festivals are the 24-day [Seattle International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_International_Film_Festival),[^cite_note-214] Northwest Folklife over the [Memorial Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Day) weekend, numerous [Seafair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seafair) events throughout July and August (ranging from a [Bon Odori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bon_Odori) celebration to the [Seafair Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seafair_Cup) [hydroplane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroplane_\(boat\)) races), the [Bite of Seattle](726657.md), one of the largest [Gay Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Pride) festivals in the United States, and the art and music festival [Bumbershoot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bumbershoot), which programs music as well as other art and entertainment over the Labor Day weekend. All are typically attended by 100,000 people annually, as are the [Seattle Hempfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Hempfest) and two separate [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(United_States\)) celebrations.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]

Other significant events include numerous Native American [pow-wows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pow-wow), a Greek Festival hosted by [St. Demetrios Greek Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Demetrios_Greek_Orthodox_Church_\(Seattle\)) in [Montlake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montlake,_Seattle), and numerous ethnic festivals (many associated with [Festál at Seattle Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fest%C3%A1l_at_Seattle_Center)).[^cite_note-219]

There are other annual events, ranging from the Seattle Antiquarian Book Fair & Book Arts Show;[^cite_note-220] an [anime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anime) convention, [Sakura-Con](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakura-Con);[^cite_note-221] [Penny Arcade Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Arcade_Expo), a gaming convention;[^cite_note-222] a two-day, 9,000-rider [Seattle to Portland Bicycle Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_to_Portland_Bicycle_Classic);[^cite_note-223] and specialized film festivals, such as the [Maelstrom International Fantastic Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maelstrom_International_Fantastic_Film_Festival), the [Seattle Asian American Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Asian_American_Film_Festival), Children's Film Festival Seattle, Translation: the Seattle Transgender Film Festival, the [Seattle Queer Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Queer_Film_Festival), Seattle Latino Film Festival, and the [Seattle Polish Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Polish_Film_Festival).[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225]

The [Henry Art Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Art_Gallery) opened in 1927, the first public art museum in Washington.[^cite_note-226] The [Seattle Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Art_Museum) (SAM) opened in 1933 and moved to their current downtown location in 1991 (expanded and reopened in 2007); since 1991, the 1933 building has been SAM's [Seattle Asian Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Asian_Art_Museum) (SAAM).[^cite_note-227] SAM also operates the [Olympic Sculpture Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Sculpture_Park) (opened in 2007) on the waterfront north of the downtown piers.[^cite_note-228] The [Frye Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frye_Art_Museum) is a free museum on [First Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Hill,_Seattle).[^cite_note-229]

Regional history collections are at the Log House Museum in Alki, [Klondike Gold Rush National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klondike_Gold_Rush_National_Historical_Park), the [Museum of History and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_History_and_Industry), and the [Burke Museum of Natural History and Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burke_Museum_of_Natural_History_and_Culture). Industry collections are at the [Center for Wooden Boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Wooden_Boats) and the adjacent [Northwest Seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Seaport), and the [Museum of Flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Flight). Regional ethnic collections include the [National Nordic Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Nordic_Museum), the [Wing Luke Asian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wing_Luke_Asian_Museum), and the [Northwest African American Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_African_American_Museum). Seattle has artist-run galleries,[^cite_note-Scott2006-230] including ten-year veteran Soil Art Gallery,[^cite_note-231] and the newer Crawl Space Gallery.[^cite_note-232]

The [Seattle Great Wheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Great_Wheel), one of the largest [Ferris wheels](175541.md) in the US, opened in June 2012 as a new, permanent attraction on the city's waterfront, at [Pier 57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier_57_\(Seattle\)), next to [Downtown Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Seattle).[^cite_note-great_wheel_opens-233] The [Seattle Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Aquarium) opened on the downtown waterfront in 1977 and was expanded in 2007 with an auditorium, gift shop, and cafe alongside new exhibit spaces. A new, three-story building under the [Overlook Walk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overlook_Walk) opened in 2024 with tropical exhibits and a 500,000-US-gallon (1,900,000 L) tank with sharks and rays from the [Coral Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coral_Triangle) region of Southeast Asia.[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235]

[Woodland Park Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodland_Park_Zoo) opened as a private [menagerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menagerie) in 1889 but was sold to the city in 1899.[^cite_note-236] The city also has many [community centers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_centers_in_Seattle) for recreation, including Rainier Beach, Van Asselt, Rainier, and Jefferson south of the Ship Canal and Green Lake, Laurelhurst, Loyal Heights north of the Canal, and Meadowbrook.[^cite_note-237] The [Seattle Underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Underground) Tour is an exhibit of places that existed before the Great Fire and subsequent rebuilding of modern-day Pioneer Square, which raised the street level.[^cite_note-238]

Since the mid-1990s, Seattle has experienced significant growth in the cruise industry, especially as a departure point for Alaska cruises. In 2023, a record total of 907,572 cruise passengers passed through the city, surpassing the number for Vancouver, BC, the other major departure point for Alaska cruises.[^cite_note-239] New tourist industries, such as guided tours and [amphibious tours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duck_tour) also emerged during the 1990s.[^cite_note-240]

### Libraries

Main article: [Seattle Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Public_Library)



[Seattle Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Central_Library)

The [Seattle Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Public_Library) system consists of 27 branches with a combined total of 3,119,298 items as of 2023.[^cite_note-241] The library was founded as part of the city government in 1890, though previous efforts to establish one date back to 1868; it first opened in 1891 and moved into a permanent location, a [mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansion) once owned by Seattle pioneer [Henry Yesler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Yesler), in 1899.[^cite_note-SPL_central_1906_-_HL-242] The mansion burned down in 1901 along with most of the 33,000 books then in the library's collection, resulting in the construction of a [Carnegie library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_library) building in 1906; eventually known as the [Seattle Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Central_Library), the building was replaced in 1960 with an [International Style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Style) design and again in 2004 with a design by Dutch architect [Rem Koolhaas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rem_Koolhaas).[^cite_note-SPL_central_1906_-_HL-242][^cite_note-243]

## Religion

See also: [List of places of worship in Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_places_of_worship_in_Seattle)

A 2024 Household Pulse Survey from the United States Census Bureau estimated that 64 percent of adults in the Seattle area never attend religious services or attend less than once a year, the highest percentage among large U.S. metropolitan areas.[^cite_note-244]

According to a 2024 study by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), the largest religious groupings in the metropolitan area are [Christians](5211.md) (44%) and those who identify with [no religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) (44%). They are followed by [Buddhists](3267529.md) (4%), [Muslims](6037917.md) (2%), [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (1%), [Jews](15624.md) (1%), and a variety of other religions have smaller followings.[^cite_note-Pew2025-245] According to the same study, about 32% of Seattleites are [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant), and 11% are [Roman Catholic](606848.md). Meanwhile, 14% of the residents in Seattle call themselves [agnostics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnostics), while 9% call themselves [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheists).[^cite_note-Pew2025-245]

Notes
